RippleNet adaptively identifies forgery-sensitive regions and constructs multi-directional, multi-scale differential representations within local neighborhoods, explicitly characterizing anomalous patterns in neighborhood statistics. More importantly, we refine the attention mechanism to operate within the local differential representation space, enabling the model to establish explicit dependencies at a finer statistical granularity. This design facilitates the capture of pixel-level forgery traces that are difficult to model using conventional convolutions or image-wide patch-level attention. Extensive experiments on multiple public benchmarks and under cross-generator evaluation settings demonstrate that RippleNet achieves consistently competitive performance.

</details>


### [25] [LocusGS: Spatially Grounded Tokens for Feed-Forward 3D Gaussian Splatting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12825)
*Wenyu Li,Sidun Liu,Tongrui Hu,Peng Qiao,Yong Dou*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: LocusGS augments each Gaussian query in query-based 3D Gaussian Splatting with a 3D anchor (center + radius), enabling progressive refinement and anchor-guided decoding to enforce local, spatially coherent Gaussians.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing Gaussian queries are purely latent and their Gaussians from a single query can scatter across distant regions, leading to weak spatial coherence and misalignment with scene structure.

Method: Introduce an anchor state per Gaussian query, refined across decoder layers and used in query interaction, multi-view feature aggregation, and Gaussian generation. Use an anchor-to-ray geometric bias to steer queries toward relevant image observations and center-based decoding to organize Gaussians within a local region.

Result: On novel view synthesis benchmarks, LocusGS improves rendering quality over query-based Gaussian baselines with the same Gaussian budget; learned anchors form coherent spatial layouts and yield more structured Gaussian distributions.

Conclusion: Explicit anchor states effectively improve the spatial organization and coherence of Gaussian queries in 3DGS, with demonstrated gains and a public project page for replication.

Abstract: Recent query-based feed-forward 3DGS methods represent a scene using learnable queries, each aggregating multi-view evidence and decoding a group of Gaussians. Ideally, different queries should specialize in coherent local regions of the scene. However, we observe that Gaussians decoded from the same query often scatter across distant scene regions, resulting in weak query-level spatial coherence and poor alignment with the scene structure. We attribute this behavior to the purely latent representation of existing Gaussian queries. To address this limitation, we introduce LocusGS, which augments each Gaussian query with a 3D anchor state consisting of a center and a support radius. The anchor state is progressively refined across decoder layers and is used throughout query interaction, multi-view feature aggregation, and Gaussian generation. Specifically, an anchor-to-ray geometric bias guides each query toward spatially relevant image observations, while anchor-centered decoding organizes its Gaussians within a local region. Experiments on novel view synthesis benchmarks show that LocusGS improves rendering quality over query-based Gaussian token baselines under the same Gaussian budget. Further analysis shows that the learned anchors form coherent spatial layouts and lead to more structured Gaussian distributions, demonstrating that explicit anchor states improve the spatial organization. Our project page: https://leo-frank.github.io/LocusGS_viewer.

</details>


### [26] [Validation of Smartphone-Based Photogrammetric 3D Body Scanning for Automated Anthropometric Measurements Compared with a Commercial Depth-Sensor-Based Body Scanner](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12827)
*Ruting Cheng,Boyuan Feng,Chuhui Qiu,Joaquin A. Calderon,Qing Pan,Yufan Liu,James K. Hahn*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Smartphone photogrammetry (PolyCam) validated against a commercial scanner (Fit3D) for whole-body measurements in 144 pregnant participants; good agreement across four landmarks, with biases <16 mm, ICC >0.8, Pearson >0.9; longitudinal changes captured similarly; mannequin validation shows <3.5 mm bias; supports smartphone-based 3D body-shape assessment as accessible alternative.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To validate the accuracy and reliability of smartphone-based photogrammetric 3D body scanning for whole-body measurements and longitudinal monitoring, as a low-cost alternative to commercial scanners.

Method: Compare PolyCam smartphone photogrammetry to Fit3D ProScanner in 144 pregnant participants scanned longitudinally. Develop an automatic circumference extraction pipeline for four anatomical landmarks from paired 3D scans. Use a linear mixed-effects model to assess scanner effects and longitudinal body-shape changes. Assess measurement consistency with repeated PolyCam scans and mannequin comparisons against tape measurements.

Result: PolyCam demonstrated strong agreement with Fit3D: average biases <16 mm, ICC >0.8, Pearson >0.9 across landmarks. Longitudinal body-shape changes were comparable between systems. Mannequin experiments showed mean biases <3.5 mm and no significant differences from tape measurements.

Conclusion: Smartphone photogrammetry can be a viable, accessible alternative to commercial body scanners for longitudinal 3D body-shape assessment, with validated performance in a pregnant cohort.

Abstract: 3D body scanning has become an important tool in healthcare applications because of its rapid and non-invasive nature. While smartphone-based photogrammetric reconstruction provide a low-cost and accessible alternative to commercial 3D body scanners, their performance for whole-body scanning remains insufficiently validated. Thus, we designed this study to comprehensively validate the photogrammetric 3D scanning application by evaluating automatically extracted whole-body measurements and longitudinal body-shape monitoring. We evaluated a representative application, PolyCam, against the commercial depth-sensor-based Fit3D ProScanner using 144 pregnant participants scanned longitudinally throughout pregnancy. We designed an automatic circumference extraction pipeline to get measurements at four anatomical landmarks from paired 3D scans. A linear mixed-effects model was used to evaluate scanner effects and longitudinal body-shape changes. Measurement consistency was assessed using repeated PolyCam scans and tape measurements on a rigid mannequin. PolyCam demonstrated strong agreement with Fit3D, with average biases below 16 mm, intraclass correlation coefficients above 0.8, and Pearson correlation coefficients above 0.9 across all landmarks. Both systems captured comparable longitudinal body-shape changes. Mannequin experiments showed mean biases below 3.5 mm and no significant differences from tape measurements. These findings support smartphone photogrammetry as a potential accessible alternative to commercial body scanners and applicable for longitudinal 3D body-shape assessment.

</details>


### [27] [EgoPHI: Estimating Contact and Force from Egocentric Vision](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13014)
*Andela Ilic,Rachel Schuchert,Yijing Jiang,Christian Holz*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: EgoPHI jointly estimates dense hand-object contact maps and 3D force distributions from a single monocular RGB image and object geometry, using a physics-based simulation pipeline to provide dense per-vertex force supervision; it outperforms prior force-estimation methods, generalizes to unseen datasets, and demonstrates sim-to-real transfer on real objects with multiple participants.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Understanding egocentric hand-object interaction requires reasoning about physically grounded forces, not just contact localization. The lack of scalable ground-truth force data motivates a physics-based data augmentation strategy to supervise dense per-vertex forces across hand and articulated object meshes.

Method: EgoPHI takes monocular RGB images and object geometry to predict dense 3D contact maps and per-vertex force distributions on interacting hand and articulated object meshes. A physics-based simulation pipeline augments existing datasets with dense force supervision, enabling training for dense contact/force estimation across meshes. Evaluation includes in-distribution, out-of-distribution, sim-to-real transfer with physical objects and multi-participant data.

Result: EgoPHI improves force estimation over existing approaches on both in-distribution and out-of-distribution benchmarks and generalizes to unseen datasets. In sim-to-real tests, two physical objects capture dense object contact and force magnitude; eight participants provide diverse touches and grasps. The method recovers meaningful 3D contact and force distributions across simulated, out-of-distribution, and real-world settings.

Conclusion: This work advances egocentric hand-object understanding from mere contact localization toward physically grounded interaction reasoning, demonstrating effective sim-to-real transfer and broad generalization.

Abstract: Understanding hand-object interaction from egocentric vision is essential for modeling how people physically engage with the surrounding world. Yet reasoning about physically grounded interaction requires estimating the forces acting on hands and objects, beyond localizing contact. We present EgoPHI, the first method that jointly estimates dense contact maps and 3D force distributions on hand and object meshes from a single monocular RGB image and object geometry. To address the lack of scalable ground-truth force annotations, we introduce a physics-based simulation pipeline that augments existing hand-object datasets with dense per-vertex force supervision. EgoPHI then learns dense 3D contact and force on interacting hand and articulated object meshes, extending vision-based force estimation beyond image-space or planar settings. Our evaluation on in-distribution and out-of-distribution benchmarks shows that EgoPHI improves force estimation over existing approaches while generalizing to unseen datasets. To evaluate sim-to-real transfer, we constructed two physical objects that capture dense object contact and force magnitude and used them to record a dataset of interactions from eight participants across diverse touch and grasp types. Our results demonstrate that EgoPHI recovers meaningful 3D contact and force distributions in simulated, out-of-distribution, and real-world settings, advancing egocentric hand-object understanding from contact localization toward physically grounded interaction reasoning.

</details>


### [28] [Semantic Steering for Controllable Generation: Tuning-Free Concept Erasure in Multimodal Diffusion Transformers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12829)
*Qiao Li,Xiaomeng Fu,Yuanshu Zhao,Qipeng Wang,Jiao Dai,Jizhong Han*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Erases undesirable concepts in multimodal diffusion transformers (MM-DiTs) by a training-free manipulation of internal representations: extract concept vectors from middle blocks, form a steering vector from their difference, and inject it into early/middle blocks via sparse text tokens, achieving state-of-the-art erasure with negligible overhead and robustness to adversarial attacks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: MM-DiTs pose safety risks due to powerful text-to-image generation. Existing parameter-based or tuning-free erasure methods are often architecture-specific, impractical for large deployed models, or ineffective due to deep knowledge embedding and broad semantic spaces. A training-free, model-internal intervention that is architecture-agnostic and scalable is highly desirable for safe deployments.

Method: Perform a block-wise analysis of MM-DiT's generative roles and identify middle blocks as most salient for text-conditioned semantics. Extract internal representations for an unwanted concept and a safe concept from the middle block. Construct a steering vector from their difference. Inject this vector into consecutive early and middle blocks, operating solely on sparse text-branch tokens. Leverage the straight sampling trajectory of rectified flow to enable erasure without training and with minimal overhead.

Result: Across MM-DiT models, the approach achieves state-of-the-art concept erasure, enables effective control over final outputs, and demonstrates robustness to adversarial attacks, all with negligible computational overhead and without any model training.

Conclusion: Internal-representation steering provides a practical, training-free, and architecture-agnostic pathway to erase concepts in MM-DiTs, balancing safety and output controllability while maintaining efficiency.

Abstract: Multimodal Diffusion Transformers (MM-DiTs) have demonstrated remarkable text-to-image generation performance, surpassing traditional U-Net-based diffusion models. Nevertheless, their powerful generative capabilities also raise significant safety concerns, as they may generate sensitive or inappropriate content. While existing concept erasure methods aim to mitigate such risks, most require modifying model parameters, which are often architecture-specific and impractical for deployed larger models. Several tuning-free approaches face challenges when applied to advanced large-scale MM-DiTs due to their deeply embedded knowledge, broad semantic space, and context-dependent text encoders. To address these challenges, we propose to erase concepts by directly manipulating the model's internal representations. Our key insight, derived from an in-depth analysis of MM-DiT's block-wise generative roles, is that text-conditioned semantic representations are most salient in the middle blocks of MM-DiTs. Based on this, we extract representations of an unwanted concept and a desirable safe one from the middle block, construct a steering vector from their difference, and inject this single vector into consecutive early and middle blocks. By operating exclusively on the sparse text-branch tokens and leveraging the straight sampling trajectory of rectified flow, our method achieves effective concept erasure with negligible overhead and without any training. Extensive experiments across MM-DiT models demonstrate that our method achieves state-of-the-art performance in erasing diverse concepts, enables effective control over the final output, and remains robust to adversarial attacks.

</details>


### [29] [RGB-D Video Generation for Improving Human-to-Robot Object Handover Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13028)
*Tianyu Sun,Zhoujie Fu,Zihui Gao,Bang Zhang,Guosheng Lin*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Hand2Bot provides an RGB-D handover dataset with rich contextual cues, while PassGen synthesizes realistic handover sequences via diffusion and an intention-aware facial encoder, plus depth-noise editing to bridge sim-to-real gaps; results show robust zero-shot transfer and earlier intention anticipation for H2R handover.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The progress of human-to-robot handover is limited by scarce large-scale, human-centric datasets and a substantial sim-to-real gap. A realistic, noisy, context-rich dataset is needed to train robust, socially aware H2R systems.

Method: Construct Hand2Bot, an RGB-D video dataset capturing handover scenarios with body posture and facial expressions under real-world noise. Develop PassGen, a generative pipeline that uses stable video diffusion and an Intention-Aware Temporal Face Encoder to synthesize coherent handover sequences with hand-object consistency. Apply a morphology-based depth editing strategy to replicate realistic depth sensor noise. Evaluate through ablations and real-world robot deployment, focusing on intention identification accuracy and false trigger rates, demonstrating zero-shot transfer and earlier intention anticipation against hand-centric baselines.

Result: Showcases high intention identification accuracy and low false trigger rates in both ablations and real-world robot deployment. Demonstrates robust zero-shot transfer and earlier intention anticipation when trained on PassGen, enabling socially aware robot behavior in shared workspaces.

Conclusion: Training on PassGen enables robust zero-shot transfer and earlier intention anticipation for H2R handover, advancing socially aware robotic behavior while mitigating the sim-to-real gap.

Abstract: Human-to-robot (H2R) object handover is a fundamental capability for human-robot collaboration, yet progress is hindered by the scarcity of large-scale, human-centric datasets and the significant sim-to-real gap. To address these challenges, we introduce Hand2Bot, an RGB-D video dataset that provides rich contextual information such as body posture and facial expressions, specifically collected for handover scenarios with real-world noise patterns. We further propose PassGen, a generative pipeline that leverages stable video diffusion and an Intention-Aware Temporal Face Encoder to synthesize realistic handover sequences while ensuring hand-object consistency. To bridge the sim-to-real gap, we implement a morphology-based depth editing strategy that replicates realistic sensor noise found in physical depth maps. Experimental evaluations demonstrate that our framework achieves high intention identification accuracy and low false trigger rates in both ablation studies and real-world deployment on a physical robot platform. Our results confirm that training on PassGen allows for robust zero-shot transfer and earlier intention anticipation compared to traditional hand-centric baselines, effectively enabling socially aware robotic behavior in shared workspaces.

</details>


### [30] [Heterogeneous Vision-Language Ensemble with Disagreement-Aware Reranking for Text-Based Person Anomaly Retrieval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12843)
*Huu-An Vu,Cam Tu Tran Thi,Thanh Toan Le Ngo,Hoang Vo,Do Trung Hieu,Hieu Dinh Trung Pham,Khang Minh Le,Huy Minh Nhat Nguyen*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Ensemble-based cross-modal retrieval for text-based pedestrian anomaly; integrates multiple vision-language models with score alignment and disagreement-aware reranking, achieving top metrics on the Pedestrian Anomaly Behavior benchmark (PAB).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Address the challenge of fine-grained cross-modal reasoning required for text-based retrieval of anomalous pedestrians, which demands integrating appearance, behaviors, interactions, and scene context beyond traditional retrieval.

Method: 1) Use a strong retrieval backbone. 2) Progressive integration of heterogeneous vision-language embeddings via score alignment to calibrate cross-model outputs. 3) Iterative ensemble fusion to combine embeddings. 4) Disagreement-aware vision-language model (VLM) reranking to resolve ambiguity in queries.

Result: On the official PAB benchmark, the method achieves 90.92% mAP, 85.13% Recall@1, 97.72% Recall@5, and 98.68% Recall@10, indicating effective use of complementary VLM representations and selective multimodal reasoning.

Conclusion: Combining diverse vision-language representations with selective reasoning and disagreement-aware reranking yields state-of-the-art performance for large-scale text-based person anomaly retrieval, demonstrating robustness and scalability across complex, cross-modal queries.

Abstract: Text-based person anomaly retrieval aims to retrieve pedestrians exhibiting anomalous behaviors from a large image gallery using natural language descriptions. Compared with conventional text-based person retrieval, this task requires fine-grained reasoning over pedestrian appearance, behaviors, object interactions, and scene context, making robust cross-modal matching significantly more challenging. This paper presents the GENAI4E team's solution to AI City Challenge 2026 Track 4. Our framework builds upon a strong retrieval backbone and progressively integrates heterogeneous vision-language embedding models through score alignment and iterative ensemble fusion, followed by disagreement-aware VLM reranking for ambiguous queries. On the official Pedestrian Anomaly Behavior (PAB) benchmark, our approach achieves 90.92% mAP, 85.13% Recall@1, 97.72% Recall@5, and 98.68% Recall@10, demonstrating the effectiveness of combining complementary vision-language representations with selective multimodal reasoning for large-scale text-based person anomaly retrieval.

</details>


### [31] [DreamX-Phi 1.0: Action-Conditioned Video World Model for Robotic Manipulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13489)
*DreamX Team,Rui Chen,Xiangxiang Chu,Geng Li,Jifan Li,Qingfeng Shi,Datao Tang,Jing Tang,Jun Wang,Pengfei Zhang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: DreamX-Phi 1.0 is an action-conditioned video world model for robotic manipulation that predicts future observations from an observed frame, a language instruction, and a prescribed action sequence. It enforces arm identity and rigid-motion via per-arm SE(3) transformations in attention (PRoPE-style encoding), adds a depth branch for scene geometry, uses SAM3 masks with a frozen V-JEPA teacher for object consistency, and distills to a few steps for efficiency. It achieves top placements in WorldArena 2.0 Track 1 and 2, with code to be released.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Realism in predictions does not guarantee faithfulness to commanded actions and object manipulations. Predictions must respect each arm's commanded path and preserve object identity across frames, even as small manipulated objects evolve.

Method: An action-conditioned video world model that takes an observed frame, a language instruction, and an action sequence (end-effector poses and gripper states) to predict future observations. Key contributions include per-arm SE(3) transformations injected into attention via PRoPE-style geometric encoding to preserve arm identity and rigid-motion structure, a lightweight depth branch for scene geometry, SAM3 masks with a frozen V-JEPA teacher to maintain object consistency during grasping, and distribution-matching distillation to compress a multi-step generator into a few-step student for efficient deployment.

Result: Empirical results claim leading performance on Track 1 and second place on Track 2 of the WorldArena 2.0 Challenge; the authors also commit to releasing code publicly.

Conclusion: The approach integrates geometric priors, depth-based geometry, and object-consistency mechanisms with distillation to deliver faithful, efficient multi-frame predictions in robotic manipulation tasks, showing strong challenge performance and potential applicability to real-world planning and simulation pipelines.

Abstract: We present \textbf{DreamX-Phi 1.0}, an action-conditioned video world model for robotic manipulation that, given an observed frame, a language instruction, and a prescribed action sequence comprising end-effector poses and gripper states, predicts the resulting future observations. Yet realism alone does not guarantee faithfulness: a convincing rollout can still move the wrong arm or lose the manipulated object. To ensure the prediction respects each arm's commanded path, we inject per-arm $\mathrm{SE}(3)$ transformations into attention via \textbf{PRoPE-style geometric encoding}, preserving arm identity and rigid-motion structure. Action control alone does not fully constrain scene geometry or the evolution of small manipulated objects. We therefore add a lightweight \textbf{depth branch} for scene-level geometry and use \textbf{SAM3 masks} with a frozen \textbf{V-JEPA teacher} to maintain object consistency throughout grasping. We further distill the multi-step generator into a few-step student via distribution-matching distillation for efficient deployment. At the time of writing, \model{} achieves first place on Track~1 and second place on Track~2 of the WorldArena~2.0 Challenge. Our model and code will be publicly available.

</details>


### [32] [SPARED: Reasoning-Based AI-Generated Image Detection via Adversarially Edited Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12876)
*Yicheng Bao,Xiahui Guo,Xuhong Wang,Xin Tan*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Adversarial RL framework co-evolves an image editor and a reasoning detector to produce robust, explainable AI-generated image detectors that avoid data shortcuts and overfitting to static forgery corpora, with monotonic gains across rounds on external benchmarks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome three failure modes of current detectors: provenance shortcuts from heterogeneous sources, reliance on templated rationales in explanations, and stagnation due to a static forgery corpus; aim for detectors that generalize and provide grounded explanations.

Method: Two-player, alternating RL loop: a diffusion-based image editor crafts edits converting real photos into forgeries that fool the detector; a reasoning multimodal LLM provides a verdict with free-form explanations; rewards are shortcut-resistant (attacker only rewarded for faithful edits; defender only rewarded when verdict is correct). The attacker continually regenerates harder examples to probe blind spots, forcing generalization; explanations improve as a side effect of accuracy training.

Result: Detector shows monotonic improvement across rounds on three external benchmarks, indicating enhanced generalization and robustness to evolving forgeries; explanations, while not rewarded, improve in quality as a byproduct of training.

Conclusion: Adversarial co-evolution with shortcut-resistant rewards yields more robust, generalizable detectors capable of justifying decisions; the approach reduces reliance on fixed artifact distributions and templated explanations, though it may incur higher computational cost and requires careful assessment of explanation quality and real-world deployment.

Abstract: Detecting AI-generated images is only half the task: a deployed detector must also justify its verdict, yet existing detectors inherit three failure modes from their training data: real and fake images collected from different sources invite provenance shortcuts, supervised explanation corpora teach templated rationales, and a static forgery corpus leaves the decision boundary standing still while generators keep moving. We introduce \methodname{}, an adversarial reinforcement learning framework that pits two heterogeneous models against each other. A diffusion image editor learns to edit real photographs into fake counterparts of those same photographs that fool the current detector, while a reasoning MLLM learns to expose them with a verdict grounded in free-form reasoning. Both rewards are shortcut-proof by design: the attacker is credited only when its edit is faithfully executed, and the defender only when its verdict is correct. As the two models alternate, each round's attacker regenerates a harder training pool aimed at the current detector's blind spots, so the detector must generalize rather than memorize any fixed artifact distribution. Although the explanation is never rewarded, its quality rises round over round as a side effect of accuracy-only training. A detector trained within this loop improves monotonically across rounds on each of three external benchmarks.

</details>


### [33] [NaviDC-OCR: Navigating Document Parsing Across Digital and Camera-Captured Documents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12898)
*Peng Cai,Zhaofan Zou,Shifa Liu,Yikun Wang,Jiawei Tang,Kaicheng Yang,Meng Tong,Zhongjiang He,Hao Sun*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: NaviDC-OCR is a unified VLM-based document parsing framework that addresses layout sensitivity and hallucinations in high-resolution scenes via deformation-aware learning, adaptive layout sampling, and content-structure decoupled learning, achieving state-of-the-art results on several benchmarks and top placement in ICDAR 2026 Sci-ImageMiner.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome cascading errors from explicit layout analysis and repetitive hallucinations in end-to-end VLM parsing, while preserving efficiency and robust structural reasoning in complex documents.

Method: Introduce deformation-aware learning to embed geometric perception into VLMs, propose adaptive sampling for complex layouts, and employ a content-structure decoupled learning strategy to explicitly model formulas and table structures.

Result: Achieves state-of-the-art performance on OmniDocBench v1.6 (96.87), Wild-OmniDocBench (88.53), PureDocBench (78.41); ranks first in ICDAR 2026 Sci-ImageMiner Challenge; demonstrates strong generalization across diverse benchmarks.

Conclusion: NaviDC-OCR effectively integrates geometry-aware perception and structure-aware decoding within a unified framework, enabling more accurate and robust document parsing across varied scenarios.

Abstract: Document parsing aims to transform unstructured documents into structured and machine-readable representations. Recent advances in Vision-Language Models (VLMs) have significantly advanced document parsing. However, existing approaches still face two major challenges. First, decoupled VLM-based methods heavily rely on accurate layout analysis, where geometric distortions in camera-captured documents can introduce cascading errors. Second, although end-to-end VLM-based methods alleviate the dependence on explicit layout detection, they often suffer from redundant generation, hallucinations, and insufficient structural reasoning in high-resolution scenarios. To address these challenges, we propose NaviDC-OCR, a unified framework for document parsing. NaviDC-OCR introduces deformation-aware learning to incorporate geometric perception into VLMs and proposes an adaptive sampling mechanism for complex layout representation. Furthermore, a content-structure decoupled learning strategy is developed to explicitly model formula grammars and table structures, enabling more effective structured representation learning. Extensive experiments demonstrate that NaviDC-OCR achieves state-of-the-art performance across diverse document parsing benchmarks. It obtains overall scores of 96.87, 88.53 and 78.41 on OmniDocBench v1.6, Wild-OmniDocBench, and PureDocBench, respectively, and ranks first in the ICDAR 2026 Sci-ImageMiner Challenge. These results validate the effectiveness and generalization capability of NaviDC-OCR in complex document parsing scenarios.

</details>


### [34] [HounsWorld: A Multimodal World Model for Hidden Patient-State Readout, Reconstruction, and Simulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12904)
*Yunhao Bai,Zhongwei Qiu,Guangyu Guo,Yiming Huang,Tony C. W. Mok,Qinji Yu,Ling Zhang,Yan Wang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: CT-centered patient-state modeling: a 3B multimodal model (HounsWorld) and a CT-focused benchmark (HounsBench) unify readout, reconstruction, and simulation as state-driven predictions, with zero-initialized adapters and clinically meaningful CT windowing; results show strong cross-task performance and a reusable benchmark.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Clinical intelligence requires reasoning over a shared latent patient state from incomplete observations across imaging and language, rather than isolated input–output mappings. A CT-centric framework can leverage dense anatomical signals and sparse semantic notes to improve understanding, denoising, and generation.

Method: Propose a shared latent state inferred by a transformer-based world model (HounsWorld) that processes volumetric CT scans and language as observations. Outputs include: (i) query-conditioned state readouts (answers), (ii) language-based reconstructions (reports/captions), and (iii) condition-specific CT volumes for tasks like low-dose denoising, virtual contrast enhancement, and text-and-mask-to-volume generation. Introduce HounsBench with patient-disjoint splits and per-family metrics; utilize zero-initialized CT adapters to preserve pretrained mappings and use condition-explicit Hounsfield-unit window sampling. 

Result: HounsWorld demonstrates strong performance across all three task families (readout, reconstruction, and simulation) and improves CT understanding through clinically structured completion. The benchmark and model enable unified CT-language reasoning and provide an open-source implementation at the provided GitHub repo.

Conclusion: A CT-centric latent-state framework with a unified benchmark and a 3B multimodal world model effectively integrates reading, reconstruction, and generation from CT and language observations, with practical implications for denoising, contrast enhancement, and anatomy-constrained generation.

Abstract: Clinical intelligence requires estimating a patient's underlying condition from incomplete observations rather than learning isolated mappings from scans to answers. Volumetric medical images provide dense observations of anatomy, attenuation, and lesions, whereas clinical language provides sparse but complementary semantic observations. We formulate CT-centered intelligence as inference over a shared latent patient state, under which readout, reconstruction, and simulation all become state-dependent prediction problems. To operationalize this view, we introduce HounsBench, a computed tomography (CT) centric patient-state benchmark that unifies these three task families with patient-disjoint splits and per-family metrics, and HounsWorld, a 3B multimodal world model that treats volumetric scans and language as observations of the shared state through Joint Understanding-Generation Learning. A shared transformer forms an implicit patient-state estimate and supports three outputs: query-conditioned answers that read out the state, reports and captions that reconstruct it in language, and condition-specific CT volumes for low-dose denoising, virtual contrast enhancement, and anatomy-constrained text-and-mask-to-volume generation. Zero-initialized CT adapters preserve pretrained multimodal mappings, while condition-explicit Hounsfield-unit window sampling exposes clinically meaningful density observations. HounsWorld shows strong performance across all three task families while consistently improving CT understanding through clinically structured completion. Our project is available at https://github.com/byhwhite/HounsWorld.git

</details>


### [35] [Beyond Visual Evidence: Revealing and Mitigating Relational Privacy Leakage in Document MLLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12911)
*Beining Xu,Hairui Wang,Jiaxin Wang,Changsheng Chen,Anirban Chakraborty*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Introduces DRUF to decouple risky field-relations and reduce privacy leakage in KIE for domain-specific multimodal LLMs, supported by a new DocPrivacyBench benchmark; DRUF reduces leakage by 4.8 percentage points while preserving KIE performance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Domain-specific MLLMs for identity document processing exhibit privacy risks when detecting key information, especially under minimal visual evidence where models rely on memorized field-relations; a method is needed to mitigate leakage without sacrificing utility.

Method: Dynamic Relational Unlearning Framework (DRUF) comprising Relational Decoupling Unlearning (RDU) and a dynamic set update mechanism; introduces DocPrivacyBench to systematically evaluate leakage under scarce visual evidence; experimental setup assesses three MLLMs and six unlearning methods.

Result: Existing MLLMs leak privacy when visual evidence is scarce, more so on noisier datasets. DRUF achieves higher leakage suppression than baselines by about 4.8 percentage points and maintains robust KIE performance.

Conclusion: DRUF effectively mitigates privacy risks in domain-specific KIE tasks without compromising extraction accuracy; DocPrivacyBench provides a principled benchmark for evaluating privacy leakage under limited visual evidence.

Abstract: While the privacy risks of multimodal large language models (MLLMs) have drawn significant attention, the unique vulnerabilities of domain-specific MLLMs remain largely underexplored. Focusing on document understanding MLLMs for identity document processing, this paper investigates the privacy issues inherent in Key Information Extraction (KIE) tasks. We reveal that when input images lack sufficient visual evidence, these models often rely on memorized field relations from training data to infer missing content, thereby leaking multiple correlated fields containing sensitive personal information. To mitigate this risk, we make three key contributions.First, we propose the Dynamic Relational Unlearning Framework (DRUF) which comprises a Relational Decoupling Unlearning (RDU) module and a dynamic set update mechanism. It suppresses the leakage of high-risk field pairs while preserving KIE performance.Second, we introduce DocPrivacyBench, a novel benchmark to systematically evaluate a model's susceptibility to privacy leakage under conditions of absent or minimal visual evidence.Third, we evaluate three MLLMs and six unlearning methods using this benchmark, assessing both post-unlearning leakage suppression and utility preservation.Our results demonstrate that existing MLLMs consistently exhibit privacy leakage when visual evidence is scarce, particularly on noisier datasets. In contrast, DRUF outperforms the strongest baseline by improving leakage suppression by 4.8 percentage points, effectively mitigating privacy risks while maintaining robust document information extraction performance.

</details>


### [36] [TennisVAR: A Stroke-Evidence-Grounded Multimodal Large Language Model for Tactical Reasoning in Tennis Videos](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12920)
*Yifan Mei,Qingling Shi,Changli Wu,Jiayuan Rao,Jiayi Ji,Liujuan Cao*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Introduces stroke-evidence-grounded tactical reasoning for tennis rally-level understanding via TRACE benchmark and TennisVAR model; unifies perception, tactical reasoning, and evidence-grounded Q/A.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current tennis-video methods either focus on individual strokes without modeling tactical dependencies or provide high-level analyses without grounding in events; there is a need to bridge perception and understanding by grounding tactical reasoning in concrete stroke events.

Method: Proposes a new rally-level task of stroke-evidence-grounded tactical reasoning. Builds TRACE, a large-scale expert-annotated benchmark with 11,189 rally videos, 41,485 stroke events, 25,429 tactical units, and 11,189 Q/A pairs, unifying fine-grained stroke attributes, cross-stroke tactical relations, hierarchical tactic annotations, and evidence-grounded questions. Introduces TennisVAR, an evidence-grounded multimodal LLM with an event-relation-evidence-tactic reasoning paradigm. An Event Parsing Module converts rallies into explicit stroke-event sequences, and a Tactical Graph-Guided Temporal Reasoner jointly models rally progression and same-player decision dependencies to identify question-relevant evidence and decisive actions.

Result: TRACE provides a unified dataset with fine-grained stroke attributes, cross-stroke relations, hierarchical tactics, and evidence-grounded questions; TennisVAR demonstrates feasibility of event parsing and graph-guided reasoning to ground answers in concrete actions and evidence within rallies.

Conclusion: The work advances sports-video understanding by tightly coupling perception with tactical reasoning and decision-making in tennis, via a scalable benchmark and an evidence-grounded reasoning model; the approach lays groundwork for generalizing to other sports.

Abstract: Sports-video understanding is moving beyond event recognition toward explaining how actions collectively shape match progression, however, existing tennis-video methods either perceive individual strokes without modeling their tactical dependencies or generate high-level analyses without grounding them in the underlying events. To bridge this perception-to-understanding gap, we formulate stroke-evidence-grounded tactical reasoning, a new rally-level task that requires models to jointly predict an open-ended answer, a hierarchical tactic label, an ordered sequence of supporting strokes, and decisive key actions, with each evidence stroke anchored to its racket-ball contact frame. We further introduce TRACE (Tactical Reasoning with Action-Chain Evidence in Tennis), a large-scale expert-annotated benchmark containing 11,189 rally videos, 41,485 stroke events, 25,429 tactical units, and 11,189 question-answer pairs, which unifies fine-grained stroke attributes, cross-stroke tactical relations, hierarchical tactic annotations, and evidence-grounded questions across factual perception, tactical understanding, and decision reasoning. Building on TRACE, we propose TennisVAR (Tennis Video Action-chain Reasoner), an evidence-grounded multimodal large language model that follows an "event-relation-evidence-tactic" reasoning paradigm, where an Event Parsing Module converts continuous rallies into explicit stroke-event sequences while a Tactical Graph-Guided Temporal Reasoner jointly models rally progression and same-player decision dependencies to identify question-relevant evidence and decisive actions.

</details>


### [37] [A Deep RL based Framework for Targeted White Matter Tractography](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12960)
*Ankita Joshi*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A data-driven tractography framework that blends reinforcement learning with supervised learning (GPT-based policy learning) to refine tract-specific policies without ground-truth fibers and to fuse multiple policies for improved robustness and accuracy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Address the persistent challenge of false positives and ambiguity in white-matter tractography by enabling learning-based refinement without requiring ground-truth fiber annotations and avoiding explicit segmentation.

Method: Hybrid reinforcement learning with supervised (GPT-based) policy learning for tract-specific refinement; scalable data-driven multi-policy fusion combining multiple RL policies; bypasses explicit segmentation and does not rely on ground-truth fibers; validated on public benchmarks.

Result: Extensive validation on TractoInferno, HCP, and ISMRM-2015 datasets shows generalization across data sources and accurate reconstruction of brain white matter tracts; improved robustness and reliability in tractography.

Conclusion: The framework represents a significant advancement in tractography, reducing dependence on ground-truth annotations while enhancing robustness, reliability, and accuracy through tract-specific RL refinement and multi-policy fusion.

Abstract: Fiber tractography's ability to reconstruct the brain's structural pathways, has made it a crucial component of modern neuroimaging, enabling detailed, non-invasive mapping of structural connectivity and supporting a wide range of neurological research and clinical applications. However, despite its importance, tractography remains a challenging task due to the inherent complexity of white matter structure and its susceptibility to false positives, which can lead to the misrepresentation of critical pathways.
  To overcome these limitations, in this thesis, we propose a hybrid framework that integrates reinforcement learning with supervised learning for refining RL policies, specifically tailored for tract-specific tractography. Notably, our framework does not rely on ground-truth fibers for training. Moreover, the tract-specific formulation bypasses the need for an explicit segmentation process, simplifying the overall pipeline.
  Our work includes two main contributions, each building upon the previous. First, we introduce a hybrid approach that combines reinforcement learning with supervised learning (specifically, GPT-based policy learning) to refine policies in a tract-specific context. Second, we propose a scalable framework for data-driven multi-policy fusion, which leverages the complementary strengths of multiple RL policies to improve tractography performance and robustness. We demonstrate the effectiveness of our framework through extensive validation on benchmark public datasets including TractoInferno, HCP, and ISMRM-2015, highlighting its ability to generalize across data sources and accurately reconstruct brain white matter tracts. We believe that these contributions represent significant advancements in the field of tractography, improving robustness, reliability, and accuracy while reducing dependence on ground-truth annotations.

</details>


### [38] [Bias Mitigation in Face Recognition via Demographic-based Supervised Contrastive Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12971)
*Yu Linghu,Salman Mohammad,Xinyi Zhang,Manuel Günther*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Demographic-based Supervised Contrastive loss (DeSCon) improves fairness in face recognition by targeting tail behavior through demographic-aware batch composition and pair selection, outperforming simple data-balancing while retaining competitive verification accuracy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Bias in face recognition across gender, age, and ethnicity persists beyond training-data imbalance; deployment requires low false match rates at the tail of non-match distributions; aim to enhance fairness without sacrificing performance.

Method: Introduce DeSCon: a supervised contrastive loss with batch design and demographic-aware pair selection that emphasizes cross- and within-demographic relationships to improve tail discrimination; trained on demographically labeled data and standard benchmarks.

Result: Empirical results on demographically labeled datasets and verification benchmarks show improved fairness beyond data balancing, with competitive verification performance.

Conclusion: DeSCon offers a practical approach to enhance fairness in face recognition by focusing on tail behavior; code available upon request.

Abstract: Face recognition systems have been shown to be biased toward certain demographic groups by exhibiting different error rates across gender, age, or ethnicity. Though the imbalance of the training data with respect to these demographics is one cause of this bias, training on artificially balanced groups does not completely mitigate the problem. For deployment, face recognition typically works at operating points allowing very low false match rates and, hence, on the tail of the non-match score distribution. While class balancing can improve the means of these distributions, the aim of our approach is to improve fairness by addressing the behavior in the tail. Particularly, we propose the Demographic-based Supervised Contrastive loss (DeSCon) for face recognition, which relies on a well-designed composition of training batches and demographic-aware pair selection. Our experimental evaluation on both demographically-labeled datasets and standard verification benchmarks shows that DeSCon can improve fairness beyond balancing training datasets while maintaining competitive verification performance. Source code is available upon request.

</details>


### [39] [DiCoR: Decoupled Referent Disambiguation and Contour Recalibration for Efficient Referring Remote Sensing Image Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12980)
*Ziyang Gao,Zhizhuo Jiang,Jingjing Chang,Yixin Yang,Yuwen Pan,Yong-Qiang Mao,Yu Liu,Hai-Bao Chen*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: DiCoR bridges joint fusion segmentation (JFS) and decoupled prompt segmentation (DPS) for remote sensing image segmentation by introducing disambiguation-aware localization guidance and a lightweight contour recalibration module, achieving state-of-the-art accuracy with low overhead.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: RRSIS requires accurate referent localization and precise mask delineation. JFS optimizes both jointly but can sacrifice accuracy; DPS decouples localization and mask generation but incurs higher memory and latency. A decoupled approach that preserves efficiency is desirable.

Method: Propose DiCoR: a decoupled referent disambiguation and contour recalibration framework built on a JFS pipeline. It uses a disambiguation-aware localization guidance strategy to rank salient candidate regions with adaptive linguistic cues and injects the resulting localization prior into fused features. It also includes a lightweight contour recalibration module that predicts residual corrections to coarse logits under localized contour supervision to refine masks with minimal overhead.

Result: Experiments on RefSegRS, RRSIS-D, and RISBench show DiCoR achieves best segmentation accuracy across benchmarks. On RefSegRS, it improves mIoU by 5.28% and gIoU by 2.87% over a competitive JFS baseline, and runs 4.7% faster than a representative DPS method.

Conclusion: DiCoR delivers a favorable accuracy–efficiency trade-off by effectively decoupling referent localization and contour refinement within an efficient JFS framework; code is publicly available.

Abstract: Referring remote sensing image segmentation (RRSIS) aims to delineate targets specified by natural language expressions in remote sensing imagery. Existing methods mainly follow joint fusion segmentation (JFS) or decoupled prompt segmentation (DPS). JFS is efficient but often suffers from limited accuracy because referent localization and mask delineation are optimized under a unified objective, whereas DPS separates localization from mask generation using spatial prompts and foundation segmenters at the cost of higher memory consumption and inference latency. To bridge this gap, we propose DiCoR, a decoupled referent disambiguation and contour recalibration framework built on an efficient JFS pipeline. DiCoR addresses two key challenges: distinguishing the correct referent from ambiguous candidates and refining coarse masks after localization. A disambiguation-aware localization guidance strategy ranks salient candidate regions with adaptive linguistic cues and injects the resulting localization prior into fused features. A lightweight contour recalibration module further predicts residual corrections to coarse logits under localized contour supervision, improving mask quality with limited computational overhead. Experiments on RefSegRS, RRSIS-D, and RISBench show that DiCoR achieves the best segmentation accuracy across all three benchmarks. On RefSegRS, it improves mIoU and gIoU by 5.28% and 2.87% over a competitive JFS method while running 4.7% faster than a representative DPS method, demonstrating a favorable accuracy-efficiency trade-off. Code is available at https://github.com/zyGao1126/DiCoR.

</details>


### [40] [PixSDS: Why Latent SDS Makes Noisy Pixels](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12997)
*Vsevolod Skorokhodov*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: PixSDS is a lightweight repair for latent SDS that uses a decoded lookahead to steer pixel-space optimization, reducing VAE-induced artifacts without retraining diffusion models or changing the SDS objective.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Latent SDS often yields structured color artifacts and texture noise due to pixel drift along directions weakly constrained by the VAE encoder. A lightweight, VAE-consistent gradient repair is needed to preserve semantic content without retraining models.

Method: Compute a latent SDS step, decode the latent update to an image, and use this decoded image as a clean direction to guide pixel-space optimization, thereby reducing motion in VAE-inconsistent directions while keeping the original SDS objective intact.

Result: PixSDS substantially reduces structured artifacts (e.g., color artifacts, texture noise) in 2D optimization and text-to-3D generation, while preserving semantic content.

Conclusion: PixSDS is a lightweight, effective, and model-agnostic repair for latent SDS that requires no retraining or renderer/objective changes; code is publicly available.

Abstract: Score Distillation Sampling (SDS) enables text-to-3D generation by optimizing rendered images with a pretrained diffusion prior, but latent SDS often produces structured color artifacts and high-frequency texture noise. We identify a failure mode of latent SDS caused by VAE-induced pixel drift: the optimized image can move along pixel-space directions that are weakly constrained by the VAE encoder, so its latent representation remains clean and semantically meaningful while the image itself accumulates visible artifacts. We support this diagnosis with controlled 2D SDS experiments, VAE-only optimization, and a simplified analysis showing that encoder-like latent objectives can amplify image-space noise when the inverse mapping to pixels is underconstrained. Motivated by this observation, we propose PixSDS, a lightweight VAE-consistent gradient repair method. PixSDS decodes a latent SDS lookahead step and uses the decoded image as a clean direction for pixel-space optimization, reducing motion in VAE-inconsistent directions without retraining the diffusion model, changing the renderer, or replacing the SDS objective. Experiments in 2D optimization and text-to-3D generation show that PixSDS substantially reduces structured artifacts while preserving semantic content. Code is publicly available at https://sevashasla.github.io/pixsds-webpage/.

</details>


### [41] [Structure-aware Riemannian Growth Fields for 4D Plant Modeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13007)
*Meng-Yu Jennifer Kuo,Ryo Kawahara*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A continuous, structure-aware 4D plant growth model that reconstructs geometry and topology from sparse timepoints via a geodesic flow on a Riemannian growth field, bridging large temporal gaps and preserving hierarchies; plus a 10-day dual-species annotated dataset; outperforms baselines in geometry and correspondences.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Dense registration-based methods struggle with large temporal gaps due to self-occlusions and non-rigidity, making reliable dense sequences scarce. A continuous morphogenesis framework is needed to model topology evolution and geometric deformation coherently across time, preserving botanical hierarchies and stable correspondences.

Method: Formulate morphogenesis as a continuous procedural process on a structure-aware Riemannian growth field. Jointly model topology evolution and geometric deformation by grounding symbolic growth rules within a continuous geodesic flow, ensuring structural coherence under topological changes. Provide a 10-day dual-species dataset with dense geometric and semantic annotations to support evaluation.

Result: Experiments show accurate tracking of individual organ growth over time and substantial improvements over state-of-the-art baselines in geometric accuracy and correspondence consistency across distant timepoints.

Conclusion: The framework enables robust 4D plant growth modeling from sparse observations by integrating topology-aware geometric deformation with continuous morphogenesis, supported by a richly annotated dataset that enables reliable spatio-temporal correspondences and improved tracking of organ development.

Abstract: In this paper, we introduce a novel framework for 4D plant growth modeling that reconstructs the continuous geometric and topological evolution of plants from sparse temporal observations. Existing methods mainly rely on dense registration, yet reliable dense sequences are hard to obtain due to scanning constraints and self-occlusions, leaving these approaches struggling under large temporal gaps where rapid organ emergence violates local rigidity. To overcome this, we bridge these gaps by formulating plant morphogenesis as a continuous procedural process on a structure-aware Riemannian growth field; this jointly models topology evolution and geometric deformation, preserving botanical hierarchies and stable spatio-temporal correspondences across distant timepoints. Our key idea is to ground symbolic growth rules within a continuous geodesic flow, where organ development follows biologically modulated trajectories that preserve structural coherence under topological changes. We further contribute a 10-day dual-species dataset with dense geometric and semantic annotations. Experiments demonstrate that our method accurately tracks individual organ growth over time and significantly outperforms state-of-the-art baselines in both geometric accuracy and correspondence consistency.

</details>


### [42] [UniTraffic-Agent: Unified Traffic Video Reasoning for AI City Challenge 2026 Track 3 with Two Out-of-Domain Evaluations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13031)
*Peng Li,Qianqian Xu,Shilong Bao,Yangbangyan Jiang,Qingming Huang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: UniTraffic-Agent proposes a modular MR-CAS framework with an observe–reason–act–verify pipeline for traffic-video understanding, addressing Traffic Anomaly Reasoning (TAR) and out-of-domain evaluations FETV and PSI-VQA, with competitive leaderboard results and accessible code.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Traffic video understanding is challenging for multimodal LLMs due to sparse events and varied viewpoints; systems should explain how events unfold, why they happen, and when interactions occur.

Method: Introduce UniTraffic-Agent within a MR-CAS solution for Track 3 of the 10th AI City Challenge. It uses an observe–reason–act–verify workflow that samples timestamped visual evidence, reasons over all questions from the same clip in one request, and converts responses via task-specific action adapters. Evaluates on TAR, FETV (fisheye events), and PSI-VQA (pedestrian intention reasoning).

Result: On official leaderboards, MR-CAS ranks 16th on TAR (0.5780), 2nd on FETV (0.4884), and 4th on PSI-VQA (64.4161). Code available at https://github.com/Roclp/UniTraffic-Agent.

Conclusion: The work demonstrates a modular, evidence-centric approach for multimodal traffic understanding with competitive performance across standard and out-of-domain tasks, and provides an accessible implementation.

Abstract: Traffic video understanding has become an important problem in intelligent transportation, as road videos provide direct evidence for accidents, violations, and interactions between vehicles and vulnerable road users. A useful system should explain how a traffic event develops, why it happens, and when the relevant interaction occurs, yet this remains difficult for multimodal large language models (MLLMs) because traffic videos contain sparse events and varied viewpoints. We introduce UniTraffic-Agent, the MR-CAS solution for Track~3 of the 10th AI City Challenge, which includes Traffic Anomaly Reasoning (TAR) and two out-of-domain evaluations: FETV for fisheye traffic events and PSI-VQA for pedestrian intention reasoning. UniTraffic-Agent follows an observe--reason--act--verify workflow that samples timestamped visual evidence, reasons over all questions from the same clip in one request, and converts responses through task-specific action adapters. On the official Public leaderboards, MR-CAS ranks 16th on TAR with a score of 0.5780, 2nd on FETV with 0.4884, and 4th on PSI-VQA with 64.4161. The code is available at https://github.com/Roclp/UniTraffic-Agent.

</details>


### [43] [Spatially-Grounded Text-to-Video Generation via Inference-Time Gradient-Free Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13037)
*Guillaume Jeanneret,Mathis Koroglu,Hugo Caselles-Dupré,Arnaud Dapogny,Matthieu Cord*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Gradient-free, training-free method for precise spatial guidance in diffusion-based text-to-video via analytical cross-attention solution and on-the-fly latent-space injection, achieving higher localization accuracy with low overhead.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Fine-grained spatial controllability in diffusion T2V is limited; gradient-based optimization in training-free methods is computationally prohibitive on large models.

Method: Introduce an analytical, closed-form solution for cross-attention scores to guide spatial localization without backpropagation, plus an on-the-fly injection mechanism aligned with the transformer's latent-space manifold.

Result: Significant improvement in localization accuracy over existing baselines with minimal additional computational overhead.

Conclusion: GATO-Vid provides a training-free, gradient-free mechanism for precise spatial control in diffusion video generation, offering scalable and efficient localization without costly optimization.

Abstract: Diffusion Transformer Text-to-Video models have achieved remarkable synthesis quality, yet fine-grained spatial controllability remains a significant challenge. While existing training-free methods produce solid overall results in spatially grounded generation, \ie, placing a specific object in a designated location, they rely on gradient-based optimization techniques that incur prohibitive computational overhead, a bottleneck amplified in modern large-scale architectures. To address this limitation, we present Gradient-free Analytical Trajectory Optimization Video Generation (GATO-Vid), a novel training-free and gradient-free approach for precise spatial guidance. Rather than relying on costly backward passes, we introduce an alternative cross-attention score and solve it analytically to obtain an exact, closed-form solution. To use our analytical solution, we propose an on-the-fly injection mechanism tailored to the topological manifold of the transformer's latent space. Our experiments demonstrate that GATO-Vid significantly outperforms existing baselines in localization accuracy while introducing minimal computational overhead.

</details>


### [44] [P2Fusion: Prompt-based Progressive Infrared-Visible Image Fusion via Dual-Prior Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13045)
*Yi Shi,Huichao Xie,Yuqing Wang,Mingyu Wang,Kaihui Yang,Yu Liu,Ruitao Lu,Lizhe Li,Junwei Han,Dingwen Zhang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: P2Fusion is a prior-guided distillation-based infrared–visible image fusion framework using dual intrinsic prompts, Teach-to-Fuse guidance, and a GDER module to adaptively reconcile modalities, achieving state-of-the-art results on five datasets and improving downstream perception.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing IVIF methods rely on static penalties or extrinsic priors (e.g., CLIP/DINO) that fail to exploit intrinsic modality characteristics and can cause optimization conflicts. There is a need to leverage intrinsic priors—thermal saliency and spatial quality—via learnable dynamic regulators to better fuse infrared and visible features.

Method: Distill image-intrinsic priors into learnable dynamic regulators; employ a Teach-to-Fuse mechanism for dual-granularity progressive guidance; integrate a Gated Dynamic Expert Recalibration (GDER) module for decoupled feature refinement; use dual intrinsic prompts to guide fusion without hard penalties; end-to-end training with prior-guided distillation.

Result: Achieves state-of-the-art performance across five mainstream IVIF datasets, with consistent fusion-quality gains and 14/20 top metrics across 5 benchmarks; demonstrates downstream robustness, e.g., +3.2% mAP on MSRS, +0.5% on M3FD, +0.9% on DroneVehicle; code to be released at GitHub.

Conclusion: P2Fusion enables adaptive cross-modal mediation through expert specialization and dynamic priors, yielding high-fidelity fusion and robust downstream perception, with broad applicability to multimodal fusion tasks.

Abstract: Infrared-visible image fusion (IVIF) is pivotal for multimodal perception, yet reconciling the inherent information disparity between thermal and textural features remains a fundamental challenge. Existing prior-guided methods often rely on static constraints that induce optimization conflicts or utilize extrinsic semantic priors from large-scale foundation models (e.g., CLIP/DINO), which frequently fail to exploit the intrinsic modality characteristics essential for high-fidelity fusion. To address these issues, we propose P2Fusion, a prior-guided distillation-based framework that reformulates IVIF via dual intrinsic prompts. Instead of imposing hard-coded penalties, we distill image-intrinsic priors, thermal saliency and spatial quality, into learnable dynamic regulators. Specifically, a Teach-to-Fuse mechanism provides dual-granularity progressive guidance, coupled with a Gated Dynamic Expert Recalibration (GDER) module for decoupled feature refinement. This design enables the network to adaptively mediate modal competition through expert specialization. Extensive experiments demonstrate that P2Fusion achieves state-of-the-art performance across five mainstream datasets. Notably, our framework demonstrates consistent performance advantages in fusion quality, achieving state-of-the-art results in 14 out of 20 key evaluation metrics across 5 benchmarks. Furthermore, it effectively contributes to the robustness of downstream perception, such as +3.2% mAP on MSRS, +0.5% mAP on M3FD and +0.9% mAP on DroneVehicle for object detection. Our code will be available at https://github.com/YiShi99/P2Fusion

</details>


### [45] [Topology-Unified 2D Pose Estimation across Intact, Residual and Prosthetic Limbs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13047)
*Tianye Qi,Tengyue Zhang,Jiaying Ying,Tianqing Zhu,Xin Yu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: ProPose introduces a unified annotation protocol for human pose estimation (HPE) that includes prosthetics and limb absence, along with a Real-to-Synthetic data expansion to cover long-tail cases; ProLoss is a structure-aware objective that enforces dependencies within a limb to prevent non-existent joint predictions on mechanical structures; results show 2-6% improvement in long-tail prosthetic joint classification without sacrificing localization, advancing inclusive HPE for human-device interactions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Tackle representation bias in HPE benchmarks that over-represent able-bodied individuals and lack generalization to prosthetic and amputated cases; create a unified topology to capture biological limbs, prostheses, and absences; require data augmentation to address scarcity of real prosthetic images.

Method: ProPose provides a unified annotation protocol that maps biological limbs, prostheses, and absences to a single topological representation. A Real-to-Synthetic pipeline synthesizes underrepresented prosthetic scenarios to balance data. ProLoss introduces a structure-aware loss that enforces dependencies among keypoints within a limb to avoid predicting non-existent joints on mechanical structures.

Result: Empirical evaluation shows 2-6% gains in classification accuracy for long-tail prosthetic joints without sacrificing spatial localization accuracy; demonstrates improved robustness and inclusivity in HPE models.

Conclusion: Establishes a foundation for inclusive pose estimation across human bodies and assistive devices, enabling better analysis of interactions between humans and prosthetic devices and expanding downstream applications.

Abstract: Driven by the availability of large-scale datasets, Human Pose Estimation (HPE) plays a critical role in numerous downstream tasks. However, mainstream benchmarks exhibit severe representation bias, predominantly featuring able-bodied individuals. While a few pioneering datasets have attempted to address limb differences, their annotation protocols fail to generalize, struggling to represent specialized mechanical structures like running blades or unprosthetized residual limbs. To bridge this gap, we introduce ProPose, a large-scale benchmark featuring a novel annotation protocol that unifies the topological representation of biological limbs, diverse prostheses, and physical absences within a single framework. Because real-world prosthetic images are inherently scarce and exhibit extreme long-tail distributions, we design a Real-to-Synthetic data expansion pipeline to explicitly synthesize and expand the underrepresented cases. However, simply training existing models on this enriched dataset often leads to suboptimal solutions, as they estimate each keypoint independently and might hallucinate non-existent joints on mechanical structures. To resolve this, we propose ProLoss, a structure-aware objective that enforces keypoint dependencies within a single limb to prevent unrealistic limb predictions. Extensive experiments demonstrate that our approach improves the classification accuracy of long-tail prosthetic joints by 2% to 6% without compromising spatial coordinate localization performance. This work sets a foundation for inclusive pose estimation, unlocking new possibilities for understanding the interactions between human bodies and assistive devices.

</details>


### [46] [Learning Unified Video and Image Representation for Video Face Forgery Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13064)
*Haotian Liu,Yang Liu,Guoying Zhao,Xiaobai Li*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: UVIF: a unified encoder framework that leverages annotated images to detect partial (frame-level) face forgeries in videos, achieving state-of-the-art performance with no extra computational overhead.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current video forgery detectors often assume all frames are manipulated, failing on videos with partial forgeries. Incorporating fine-grained supervision from annotated images and aligning video-image distributions can improve partial forgery detection.

Method: A unified encoder (2D backbone with temporal fusion) supports both video frames and static images under a multi-task learning setup. A pseudo-labeling process links video frames to image representations, and a video-oriented feature alignment strategy narrows the distribution gap between modalities, enabling joint video–image learning without extra computation.

Result: Experimental results on benchmark datasets show that UVIF outperforms state-of-the-art methods in detecting partially forged videos, with no additional computational overhead.

Conclusion: UVIF demonstrates that cross-domain supervision from annotated images and a unified video–image encoder effectively boost partial video forgery detection.

Abstract: Face forgery detection is crucial for preserving the security and integrity of facial data given the rapid developments in face manipulation techniques and deep generative models. Existing methods for video face forgery detection typically assume that all frames in a forged video are manipulated, while detecting partially forged videos that contain only a subset of altered frames remains challenging. To address this issue, we propose a novel framework, UVIF, that utilizes additional annotated images to provide fine-grained supervision for detecting partial forgeries in videos. UVIF employs a unified encoder and a multi-task learning paradigm to jointly model facial videos and images for boosted video face forgery detection. A 2D backbone with temporal fusion modules is employed as the unified encoder. A pseudo labeling process is designed for video frames to bridge their representations with those of static images. A video-oriented feature alignment strategy is further introduced to reduce the distribution gap between videos and images. Extensive experiments on benchmark datasets demonstrate the effectiveness of our framework, which outperforms state-of-theart methods in detecting partially forged videos while introducing no additional computational overhead. Our code is available at https://github.com/haotianll/UVIF.

</details>


### [47] [Paths: Prompt-aware Spatio-temporal Transformer with Hierarchical Multi-modal Fusion for RGB-Event Video Person Re-Identification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13092)
*Yakun Huo,Yingquan Wang,Yangyang Liu,Tianyu Yan,Yunzhi Zhuge,Pingping Zhang,Huchuan Lu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Proposes Paths, a unified RE-VReID framework combining memory-augmented backbone, prompt-aware spatio-temporal transformer, and hierarchical multi-modal fusion to jointly model RGB and event streams, achieving improved performance on EvReID, MARS, and iLIDS-VID with code released.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current RGB-Event Re-ID methods often decouple spatial and temporal modeling and rely on coarse global fusion, which limits cross-modality interaction and fine-grained discriminative cue exploitation.

Method: Memory-Augmented Backbone (MAB) maintains modality-specific identity prototypes for stable intra-modal representation learning. Prompt-aware Spatio-temporal Transformer (PST) jointly models spatial and temporal cues within a single Transformer guided by prompts. Hierarchical Multi-modal Fusion (HMF) fuses RGB and event features at both global (coarse) and local (fine-grained) levels.

Result: Empirical validation on EvReID, MARS, and iLIDS-VID demonstrates the effectiveness of the proposed components and overall framework; code is available at the project repository.

Conclusion: Paths delivers robust, discriminative representations for RE-VReID by integrating unified spatio-temporal modeling with hierarchical cross-modal fusion, illustrating strong generalization across three benchmarks. Potential future work may explore efficiency, scalability, or additional modalities.

Abstract: RGB-Event Video Person Re-Identification (RE-VReID) aims to retrieve specific person across non-overlapping cameras with complementary RGB videos and event streams. However, existing methods often decouple spatial and temporal modeling, which limits their interaction. In addition, global-level RGB-Event fusion fails to fully exploit fine-grained discriminative cues. To address these issues, we propose Paths, a unified framework with spatio-temporal modeling and hierarchical multi-modal fusion for RE-VReID. Specifically, we first design a Memory-Augmented Backbone (MAB) to maintain modality-specific identity prototypes for stable intra-modal representation learning. Then, we propose a Prompt-aware Spatio-temporal Transformer (PST) to jointly model spatial and temporal cues within a unified Transformer. Finally, we introduce a Hierarchical Multi-modal Fusion (HMF) to integrate RGB and event features at global and local levels. With these modules, our framework can learn robust and discriminative representations for RE-VReID. Extensive experiments on three public RE-VReID benchmarks including EvReID, MARS and iLIDS-VID, demonstrate the effectiveness of our proposed method. The code is available at https://github.com/Reflection0427/Paths.

</details>


### [48] [RbFT-Net: Rectify-Before-Fuse Temporal Radar Anchors for 4D Radar-Camera Depth Completion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13102)
*Wentao Zhao,Shouxuan Wu,Yongtao Cen,Tianchen Deng,Yuyang Zhang,Jingchuan Wang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: RbFT-Net rectifies multi-frame 4D radar anchors before fusion to improve radar-camera depth completion, addressing sparsity, clutter, and misalignment while estimating per-point reliability.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Radar-based depth cues are sparse and noisy due to clutter, multipath, and projection errors. Temporal aggregation helps but introduces misalignment and dynamic-object interference, risking corrupted depth maps. A rectify-before-fuse approach can mitigate these issues.

Method: RbFT-Net employs an end-to-end rectify-before-fuse framework. It uses an image-conditioned rectification module to jointly correct the anchors’ image-plane locations and metric depths, while estimating per-point reliability. Rectified anchors are selectively propagated before high-level multi-modal fusion to suppress unreliable measurements.

Result: Experiments on ZJU-4DRadarCam and a new 4D radar-camera-LiDAR dataset show RbFT-Net outperforms independent radar-camera methods and is competitive with plug-in monocular depth models. Cross-platform evaluation and component analyses support the effectiveness of rectification and reliability-aware propagation.

Conclusion: Rectification and reliability-aware propagation in a rectify-before-fuse framework effectively mitigate noisy multi-frame radar cues, yielding improved dense depth completion across platforms.

Abstract: Dense metric depth prediction from cameras and millimeter-wave radar offers a cost-effective sensing solution for autonomous systems. However, radar measurements are inherently sparse and susceptible to clutter, multipath reflections, and projection errors. While aggregating multiple radar frames provides denser metric cues, it also introduces temporal misalignment and dynamic-object interference. Directly propagating such unreliable measurements can therefore corrupt large regions of the predicted depth map. To address this issue, we propose RbFT-Net, an end-to-end rectify-before-fuse framework for multi-frame 4D radar-camera depth completion. Rather than assuming accumulated radar returns to be accurate, RbFT-Net treats them as noisy temporal anchor candidates. An image-conditioned rectification module jointly corrects their image-plane locations and metric depths while estimating pointwise reliability. The rectified anchors are then selectively propagated before high-level multi-modal fusion, suppressing the influence of unreliable measurements. Experiments on ZJU-4DRadarCam and a newly collected 4D radar-camera-LiDAR dataset show that RbFT-Net consistently outperforms the evaluated independent radar-camera methods and remains competitive with plug-in pipelines using auxiliary monocular depth models. Cross-platform evaluation and component analyses further support the effectiveness of the proposed rectification and reliability-aware propagation strategy.

</details>


### [49] [Online Learning of Correspondences between Images](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13104)
*Michael Felsberg,Fredrik Larsson,Johan Wiklund,Niclas Wadströmer,Jörgen Ahlberg*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A real-time, iterative method for learning point correspondences across image sequences with unknown 3D geometry, using Neyman chi-square divergence between density representations (channel vectors) of uncertainties; updates mapping with each new image pair.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Establish accurate point correspondences when projection geometry and surface shape are unknown, without resorting to heavy global optimization or a fixed fundamental matrix; leverage sequence of pairwise observations for incremental refinement.

Method: Represent uncertainty as densities via basis functions, yielding channel vectors. Learn a mapping between these density vectors by minimizing Neyman chi-square divergence between estimated and actual location densities, updating with each new image pair for fast convergence.

Result: The algorithm runs in real-time and achieves superior convergence and accuracy compared to state-of-the-art methods in multiple experiments.

Conclusion: An iterative, density-based divergence minimization approach enables fast, accurate point correspondence learning under general imaging geometry without requiring explicit 3D reconstruction or a fixed projection model.

Abstract: We propose a novel method for iterative learning of point correspondences between image sequences. Points moving on surfaces in 3D space are projected into two images. Given a point in either view, the considered problem is to determine the corresponding location in the other view.
